# Code map

Every source file, class and function under `src/`: what it does, what it calls, what calls it and which tests reach it; which code reads and writes each input file; and the traced flows from a command to its result.

Generated 2026-10-25 from commit `e7c6549` by [tools/code_map.py](tools/code_map.py): 116 files, 1117 classes and functions, 1713 resolved calls, 7 flows. **Do not edit this file by hand.** After any code change run `python3 docs/tools/code_map.py`; it rewrites this page, [code_map.json](code_map.json), [REPO_MAP.xmind](REPO_MAP.xmind) and the interactive code map in [REPO_ATLAS.html](REPO_ATLAS.html).

How to read it:

- A name links to its line in the source.
- In *Calls* and *Called by*, a bare name lives in the same file; `module.name` lives in that module.
- Names, line numbers and descriptions are the code's own, read with Python's `ast`. Calls are resolved statically, so a call through a variable whose type the code does not name is missing: *Called by* can be incomplete, never wrong.

| [warehouse.yaml](../src/simulation_env/config/warehouse.yaml) | The one config: which map, how many robots, speed, service times, battery model, allocation window, Gazebo world name. | [W100.xlsx](../src/simulation_env/maps/W100.xlsx) | The research map, drawn in Excel: 40 x 54 cells, 552 shelves, 36 drop-offs, 24 chargers, 12 holding cells, 100 Stage Zones, one-way lane arrows. | [`warehouse_map_xlsx.load_warehouse_xlsx`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/warehouse_map_xlsx.py#L509) | no code |
| [my_map.xlsx](../src/simulation_env/maps/my_map.xlsx) | An example map made with map_tool.py example. | [`warehouse_map_xlsx.load_warehouse_xlsx`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/warehouse_map_xlsx.py#L509) | [`map_builder.write_layout_xlsx`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/map_builder.py#L335) |
| [my_map_20.xlsx](../src/simulation_env/maps/my_map_20.xlsx) | A small example map (not referenced by any doc or code). | nothing | no code |
| [my_map_60.xlsx](../src/simulation_env/maps/my_map_60.xlsx) | A medium example map (not referenced by any doc or code). | nothing | no code |

<a id="flows"></a>

## 3. Traced flows

Each flow follows one path through the code, in the order it runs. Every step names a real file and function; the generator stops if one no longer exists.

<a id="flow-build"></a>

### 3.1 Build: Excel map to config, orders and world

build_scenario.py makes a map the current one. It stops before changing anything if the map has errors.

| # | What happens | Code | Produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Read --map and the one config | [`build_scenario.main`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/build_scenario.py#L84) | map path, config path |
| 2 | Run map_tool.py check on the drawing | [`build_scenario.check_map`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/build_scenario.py#L70) | OK, or stop |
| 3 | check: load the map and run every gate | [`map_tool.cmd_check`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/map_tool.py#L116) | errors / warnings |
| 4 | Lanes, connectivity, terminal reachability, rotaries | [`map_check.check_map`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/map_check.py#L148) | a list of problems |
| 5 | Read the Excel cells, zones and lane arrows | [`warehouse_map_xlsx.load_warehouse_xlsx`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/warehouse_map_xlsx.py#L509) | WarehouseMap |
| 6 | Count the Stage Zones: the most robots the map can run | [`fleet.home_bank`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet.py#L26) | robot_count |
| 7 | Rewrite the map name and robot_count in warehouse.yaml | [`sim_config.rewrite_scenario`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/sim_config.py#L225) | config/warehouse.yaml |
| 8 | Generate 2000 orders from the map's shelves and drop-offs, seed 42 | [`generate_orders.main`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/generate_orders.py#L121) | config/orders.csv |
| 9 | Draw the warehouse as Gazebo models (the script runs top to bottom) | [`generate_gazebo_grid_world.py`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/generate_gazebo_grid_world.py) | worlds/warehouse.world |
| 10 | Turn the lanes into an RMF graph | [`generate_nav_graph.main`](../src/rmf_grid_traffic/scripts/generate_nav_graph.py#L50) | warehouse_nav_graph.yaml |
| 11 | Check the graph mirrors the lane policy | [`verify_nav_graph.main`](../src/rmf_grid_traffic/scripts/verify_nav_graph.py#L46) | OK |

<a id="flow-run"></a>

### 3.2 A headless run, start to end

run_workload.py, the same loop the ROS node runs. Every number in a report comes from these calls.

| # | What happens | Code | Produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Parse --robots --orders --strategy --seed --dataset ... | [`run_workload.main`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/run_workload.py#L44) | args |
| 2 | Read the one config | [`sim_config.load_sim_config`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/sim_config.py#L136) | SimConfig |
| 3 | Read the map | [`warehouse_map_xlsx.load_warehouse_xlsx`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/warehouse_map_xlsx.py#L509) | WarehouseMap |
| 4 | One robot per Stage Zone start cell | [`fleet.build_fleet`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet.py#L145) | list[GridRobot] |
| 5 | Read orders.csv, group rows into orders | [`orders.load_orders_csv`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/orders.py#L95) | tuple[Order] |
| 6 | Keep the first N logical orders (--orders N) | [`orders.select_orders`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/orders.py#L207) | the run's orders |
| 7 | Put them in FIFO order, all PENDING | [`order_queue.OrderQueue`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/order_queue.py#L33) | OrderQueue |
| 8 | Pick the strategy by name, with its seed | [`allocation_strategy.get_batch_strategy`](../src/task_management/task_management/allocation_strategy.py#L107) | BatchAllocationStrategy |
| 9 | Record what is being run (only with --dataset) | [`dataset.build_manifest`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/dataset.py#L353) | run.json fields, run_id |
| 10 | Build the engine, battery ledger, charger and holding banks, Stage Zone parking, route cache | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor.__init__`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L319) | WorkloadExecutor |
| 11 | Loop: dispatch, then let the engine run, until every order is done and every robot parked | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor.run`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2769) | WorkloadResult |
| 12 | Sum the robots' counters; audit every reservation | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor.totals`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2810) | makespan, distance, waiting, violations |
| 13 | The battery table | [`battery_ledger.battery_report_lines`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/battery_ledger.py#L415) | BATTERY report |
| 14 | The charger table and every charge session | [`workload_executor.charging_report_lines`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2942) | CHARGING report |
| 15 | Write the decision and outcome tables | [`dataset.RunRecorder.write`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/dataset.py#L281) | decisions.parquet, outcomes.parquet, run.json |
| 16 | The run's results | [`run_metrics.write_metrics`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/run_metrics.py#L159) | metrics.json |
| 17 | Facts and examples for ILP (with --export) | [`dataset_export.write_prolog`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/dataset_export.py#L215) | bk.pl, exs.pl |

<a id="flow-decision"></a>

### 3.3 One allocation decision

WorkloadExecutor._dispatch runs every time robots become free. Battery and charging come first, then the strategy sees the FIFO window.

| # | What happens | Code | Produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Re-try assignments that could not be planned last time | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor.retry_deferred`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1676) | missions started |
| 2 | Robots that are idle and owe nothing | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor.available_robots`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L552) | free robots |
| 3 | A robot that cannot do any pending order on its battery leaves the fleet | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._battery_gate`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1513) | CHARGE_NEEDED / CHARGE_DUE requests |
| 4 | Give every free charger to the waiting robot with the lowest SOC | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._promote`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1011) | PROMOTE |
| 5 | Every requester reserves a charger, holding cell or Stage Zone and drives off | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._evacuate`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L656) | RESERVE + charging missions |
| 6 | Each free robot's SOC and nearest charger | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._battery_context`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L601) | BatteryDecisionContext |
| 7 | The FIFO window of pending orders the strategy may see | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._candidate_window`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1794) | eligible orders |
| 8 | Count the decision's shape (robots x orders) | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._record_decision`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2121) | decision log |
| 9 | The strategy decides (here: nearest by directed A*) | [`allocation_strategy.NearestAStarAllocator.assign`](../src/task_management/task_management/allocation_strategy.py#L218) | list[OrderAssignment] |
| 10 | Score every robot-order pair | [`allocation_strategy.BatchAllocationStrategy.score`](../src/task_management/task_management/allocation_strategy.py#L156) | OrderCandidate per pair |
| 11 | Route cost and battery for each pair | [`features.build_order_candidates`](../src/task_management/task_management/features.py#L267) | OrderCandidate |
| 12 | Directed A* from the robot through the shelves to the drop-off | [`features.order_route_cost`](../src/task_management/task_management/features.py#L72) | OrderRoute (metres) |
| 13 | The real one-way route, leg by leg, with retreats | [`warehouse_routing.plan_from_cell`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/warehouse_routing.py#L1109) | MissionPlan |
| 14 | Travel + service + reach a charger + reserve | [`battery.energy_requirement`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/battery.py#L252) | EnergyRequirement |
| 15 | Take the best pairs, one order per robot | [`allocation_strategy.greedy_pairing`](../src/task_management/task_management/allocation_strategy.py#L47) | assignments |
| 16 | Re-check every returned pair independently | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._check_battery_feasibility`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1602) | refuses an ineligible pair |
| 17 | Write every pair the strategy considered (with --dataset) | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._record_candidates`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1917) | decision rows |
| 18 | Turn assignments into missions | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor.launch_batch`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2278) | missions |
| 19 | Plan the static route for one assignment | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._build_mission`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2254) | MissionPlan |
| 20 | Hand the missions to the engine | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine.assign_missions_batch`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L458) | robots start moving |

<a id="flow-engine"></a>

### 3.4 The engine: one simulated instant

FleetSimulationEngine.run calls step_once until nothing is scheduled. Robots move one cell at a time; nothing moves without a reservation.

| # | What happens | Code | Produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Step until the event queue is empty; watch for no progress | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine.run`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L1704) | simulated time advances |
| 2 | Take every event at the next time | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine.step_once`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L1626) | event batch |
| 3 | MOVE_COMPLETE, SERVICE_COMPLETE, CHARGE_COMPLETE, ... | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine._handle`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L1220) | robot states updated |
| 4 | Release the cell the robot left | [`reservations.ReservationTable.complete_move`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/reservations.py#L295) | edge and cell log |
| 5 | Robots that waited try again | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine._retry_waiting`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L1199) | move requests |
| 6 | All moves wanted at this instant, together | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine._try_moves_batch`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L677) | approved moves |
| 7 | Is the target held, or would two robots swap? | [`reservations.ReservationTable.check_move`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/reservations.py#L98) | safe / vertex / edge |
| 8 | Several robots want one cell: who goes | [`local_traffic_controller.LocalTrafficController.resolve`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/local_traffic_controller.py#L75) | MovementDecision |
| 9 | A blocked robot steps aside | [`local_yield_selector.select_local_yield_cell`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/local_yield_selector.py#L49) | yield cell |
| 10 | Claim the targets, start the moves | [`reservations.ReservationTable.begin_moves_batch`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/reservations.py#L173) | held cells |
| 11 | Count time, distance and battery for the move | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine._book_travel`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L291) | robot counters |
| 12 | Exact SOC: 1% per 20 m | [`battery_ledger.BatteryLedger.book_move`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/battery_ledger.py#L159) | SOC |
| 13 | Schedule the arrival | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine._schedule`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L235) | next event |
| 14 | After the instant: flag low batteries, retire finished orders, settle charging robots | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._tick`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2617) | dispatch again |
| 15 | An order's robot is done: record the outcome | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._retire`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2534) | COMPLETED order |
| 16 | What really happened to the order | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._record_outcome`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L2062) | outcome row |

<a id="flow-battery"></a>

### 3.5 Battery and charging

Battery V1 is fixed infrastructure, the same for every strategy. A robot asks to charge when no pending order fits its battery.

| # | What happens | Code | Produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Test every free robot against every pending order | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._battery_gate`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1513) | eligible or not |
| 2 | Is any pending order battery-feasible? | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._has_eligible_order`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1481) | yes / no |
| 3 | Leave the fleet | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._enter_request`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L636) | REQUEST event, ChargeRecord |
| 4 | Charger, else holding cell, else Stage Zone (saturation) | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._evacuate`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L656) | RESERVE |
| 5 | Cheapest free slot by directed A* | [`charging.TerminalBank.assign`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/charging.py#L476) | zone_id, cost |
| 6 | Saturation: the nearest free Stage Zone | [`stage_parking.StageParking.assign`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/stage_parking.py#L130) | Stage Zone claim |
| 7 | Arrived: occupy the terminal | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._settle`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L790) | ARRIVE |
| 8 | Charge to exactly 100% | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._begin_charge`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L829) | CHARGE_START |
| 9 | How long the charge takes | [`battery.charge_seconds_to_full`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/battery.py#L193) | seconds |
| 10 | Book the session in the engine | [`fleet_engine.FleetSimulationEngine.begin_charge`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_engine.py#L337) | charge event |
| 11 | Full: vacate the charger | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._charge_complete`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L859) | CHARGE_FULL |
| 12 | Reverse one cell into the aisle | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._reverse_off_terminal`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L890) | exit move |
| 13 | Back in the fleet | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._rejoin`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L955) | REJOIN |
| 14 | A charger frees: lowest-SOC waiting robot first (holding or Stage Zone) | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._promote`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1011) | PROMOTE |
| 15 | Reserve the charger; leave holding or the Stage Zone | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._grant_charger`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1155) | charger reserved |
| 16 | The charger trip, by directed A* | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._drive_to_charger`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1236) | TO_CHARGER |
| 17 | A CHARGE_DUE robot may cut the highest-SOC charge short | [`workload_executor.WorkloadExecutor._preempt`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/workload_executor.py#L1289) | PREEMPTED |

<a id="flow-gazebo"></a>

### 3.6 Gazebo: two terminals

Gazebo only draws what the simulator decided. The node runs the same WorkloadExecutor and paces it at 4x.

| # | What happens | Code | Produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Terminal 1: gazebo.launch.py robots:=N | [`gazebo.launch.start_gazebo`](../src/simulation_env/launch/gazebo.launch.py#L36) | launch actions |
| 2 | Stop a Gazebo already running this world | [`gazebo_world.stop_gazebo`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/gazebo_world.py#L236) | one Gazebo |
| 3 | Each robot's start cell, as the node will place it | [`gazebo_world.fleet_poses`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/gazebo_world.py#L76) | FleetPose list |
| 4 | The world plus N parked robots | [`gazebo_world.write_fleet_world`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/gazebo_world.py#L135) | /tmp/mrta_gazebo_<uid>/warehouse.world |
| 5 | The one robot model | [`gazebo_bridge.robot_model`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/gazebo_bridge.py#L317) | SDF |
| 6 | Terminal 2: orders:=true robots:=N strategy:=... order_limit:=N | [`warehouse_sim.launch.generate_launch_description`](../src/simulation_env/launch/warehouse_sim.launch.py#L58) | fleet_node with parameters |
| 7 | Start the node; the run happens inside its constructor | [`fleet_node.main`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L1146) | FleetNode |
| 8 | Load and limit the orders, the same rule as headless | [`fleet_node.FleetNode.load_workload`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L571) | OrderQueue |
| 9 | Find the robots in Gazebo (create or remove only the difference) | [`fleet_node.FleetNode.place_in_gazebo`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L364) | Gazebo already shows N robots |
| 10 | Start the C++ pose transport process | [`gazebo_bridge.FleetPoseSink`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/gazebo_bridge.py#L76) | pose stream |
| 11 | The same WorkloadExecutor as headless, with Gazebo callbacks | [`fleet_node.FleetNode.run_order_workload`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L625) | WorkloadResult |
| 12 | Each engine step: wait real time (4x) and draw | [`fleet_node.FleetNode.on_advance`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L970) | paced frames |
| 13 | Where every robot is right now, mid-move included | [`fleet_node.FleetNode._live_poses`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L1028) | FleetPose list |
| 14 | One FRAME line for all robots | [`gazebo_bridge.FleetPoseSink.send`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/gazebo_bridge.py#L117) | stdin of the C++ process |
| 15 | Set every pose in one Gazebo call | [`gazebo_fleet_pose_transport.cpp`](../src/gazebo_fleet_pose_transport/src/gazebo_fleet_pose_transport.cpp) | /world/warehouse/set_pose_vector |
| 16 | Publish state at publish_hz | [`fleet_node.FleetNode.on_tick`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L1115) | /mrta/robot_states, /mrta/fleet_status |
| 17 | Every assignment, completion and battery event | [`fleet_node.FleetNode.event`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L563) | /mrta/events |
| 18 | Short summary on the terminal, full report to a file | [`fleet_node.FleetNode.report_workload`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/fleet_node.py#L833) | ~/.ros/mrta/<run>.txt |

<a id="flow-experiment"></a>

### 3.7 Experiments, data and graphs

How a comparison of strategies is produced, and where each file comes from.

| # | What happens | Code | Produces |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | --robots 30 40 50 --seeds 1 2 3 --export | [`run_experiment.main`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/run_experiment.py#L95) | the run grid |
| 2 | Every (strategy, robots, seed); deterministic strategies once | [`run_experiment.runs_for`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/run_experiment.py#L49) | run list |
| 3 | One run_workload.py per cell, in parallel; output to run.log | [`run_experiment.run_one`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/run_experiment.py#L56) | runs/<name>/<strategy>/r<N>_s<seed>/ |
| 4 | The headless run, with --dataset | [`run_workload.main`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/run_workload.py#L44) | decisions, outcomes, run.json, metrics.json |
| 5 | One row per candidate pair at decision time | [`dataset.RunRecorder.decision`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/dataset.py#L267) | decisions.parquet |
| 6 | One row per order when its robot retires | [`dataset.RunRecorder.outcome`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/dataset.py#L276) | outcomes.parquet |
| 7 | Makespan, throughput, waiting, energy, charging... | [`run_metrics.run_metrics`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/run_metrics.py#L36) | metrics.json |
| 8 | The tables as text | [`dataset_export.write_csv`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/dataset_export.py#L144) | decisions.csv, outcomes.csv |
| 9 | Facts and pos/neg examples for ILP | [`dataset_export.write_prolog`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/dataset_export.py#L215) | bk.pl, exs.pl |
| 10 | Every run's metrics | [`plot_experiment.load`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/plot_experiment.py#L89) | rows |
| 11 | Line charts per metric vs robots | [`plot_experiment.metric_grid`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/plot_experiment.py#L137) | figures/mrta.png, traffic.png, battery.png |
| 12 | Orders completed over time | [`plot_experiment.completion_curves`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/plot_experiment.py#L205) | figures/completion_curves.png |
| 13 | Per-order duration and waiting | [`plot_experiment.order_times`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/plot_experiment.py#L237) | figures/order_times.png |
| 14 | Every metric as a table | [`plot_experiment.summary_table`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/plot_experiment.py#L287) | summary.md |

## 4. Files, layer by layer

<a id="layer-input"></a>

### 4.1 Inputs

The map, the one config, the orders and the Gazebo world. Everything starts here.

<a id="f-src-simulation-env-config-orders-csv"></a>

#### orders.csv

[`src/simulation_env/config/orders.csv`](../src/simulation_env/config/orders.csv) · 3955 lines

The order list for the current map: one row per line item (order_id, item_id, shelf_id, quantity, dropoff_id); rows sharing an order_id are one order.

- **Read by:** [`orders.load_orders_csv`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/orders.py#L95)
- **Written by:** [`generate_orders.main`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/generate_orders.py#L121)

#### warehouse.yaml

[`src/simulation_env/config/warehouse.yaml`](../src/simulation_env/config/warehouse.yaml) · 143 lines

The one config: which map, how many robots, speed, service times, battery model, allocation window, Gazebo world name. Read by every run.

- **Read by:** [`sim_config.load_sim_config`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/sim_config.py#L136), [`gazebo_world.world_name_of`](../src/simulation_env/simulation_env/gazebo_world.py#L47)

[`src/simulation_env/maps/W100.xlsx`](../src/simulation_env/maps/W100.xlsx)

The research map, drawn in Excel: 40 x 54 cells, 552 shelves, 36 drop-offs, 24 chargers, 12 holding cells, 100 Stage Zones, one-way lane arrows.

- **Read by:** [`warehouse_map_xlsx.load_warehouse_xlsx`](../src/mrta_core/mrta_core/warehouse_map_xlsx.py#L509)

#### plot_dataset.py

[`src/simulation_env/scripts/plot_dataset.py`](../src/simulation_env/scripts/plot_dataset.py) · 363 lines

<a id="f-src-rmf-grid-traffic-src-graph-check-cpp"></a>

#### graph_check.cpp

[`src/rmf_grid_traffic/src/graph_check.cpp`](../src/rmf_grid_traffic/src/graph_check.cpp) · 86 lines

Robot geometry matches the warehouse sphere model. Positive accelerations are required by RMF VehicleTraits. These values are only needed to construct traits for graph parsing. They are NOT yet part of our scientific motion model. Sphere robots do not require heading-constrained navigation.

<a id="f-src-rmf-grid-traffic-src-route-check-cpp"></a>

#### route_check.cpp

[`src/rmf_grid_traffic/src/route_check.cpp`](../src/rmf_grid_traffic/src/route_check.cpp) · 284 lines

Close all lanes entering or leaving shelf/dropoff waypoints. // The waypoints this leg actually uses as endpoints are reopened, so they can be entered and left again. // Both ends matter. Reopening only the goal made every route that *starts* on a shelf unplannable: the robot was sealed onto the cell it was standing on and the planner reported no route, even though the warehouse rule explicitly allows a one-cell retreat. // This preserves the warehouse rule: // shelf/dropoff = service endpoint only shelf/dropoff ...

<a id="f-src-rmf-grid-traffic-src-two-robot-check-cpp"></a>

#### two_robot_check.cpp

[`src/rmf_grid_traffic/src/two_robot_check.cpp`](../src/rmf_grid_traffic/src/two_robot_check.cpp) · 447 lines

This matches our old MAPF model: robots may wait on ordinary traversable cells. // Shelf/dropoff cells remain service endpoints only. RMF full planning requires positive acceleration limits. These are temporary traffic-test values. // They do NOT replace the project's scientific constant-speed execution model. In-process traffic schedule. ---------------------------------------------------------- Robot A ---------------------------------------------------------- ...

<a id="layer-strategy"></a>

### 4.5 Strategies

The research variable: how robots are paired with orders.

<a id="f-src-task-management-task-management-allocation-strategy-py"></a>

#### allocation_strategy.py

[`src/task_management/task_management/allocation_strategy.py`](../src/task_management/task_management/allocation_strategy.py) · 399 lines

The strategies: nearest by directed A*, nearest by Manhattan, random. Each scores robot-order pairs, drops battery-ineligible ones and pairs greedily.

> Batch MRTA allocation strategies for warehouse orders.

- **Imports:** [battery.py](#f-src-mrta-core-mrta-core-battery-py), [features.py](#f-src-task-management-task-management-features-py)
